What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally scared me into making this appointment is… I’ve been having thoughts like, if I didn’t wake up tomorrow, maybe that would be easier. I don’t want to hurt myself, but having that thought at all really shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling… like the color’s already drained out of the day before I even get dressed for work.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent period, have there been long stretches of time when you did not feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, before this I had stretches where I felt more like myself. But this low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now. It’s long enough that things that usually helped me—like my art classes or morning walks—just haven’t really been doing much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still try to do the things I usually like—like setting out my paints or going to pottery class—but once I’m there, I just kind of sit and stare at everything. It doesn’t give me that spark or enjoyment it used to.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen around the time this low mood began?\n\nPatient: Yes. It seemed to start around the time things got difficult with the new district manager. The whole up-and-down with my mood and energy has been going on about two months now, since that trouble really started.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that this low mood is mainly because of the problems with your new district manager?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it’s mainly because of him. The problems with the district manager definitely seemed to line up with when I started feeling worse, but I don’t know if that’s the whole reason.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I can’t think of another clear stretch before this where I felt depressed like this for two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a clear change in my sleep. It’s not something that stands out to me, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed much change in my appetite. It seems about the same to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Even if I’ve been in bed all night, I still feel worn out. Small things at work, like opening the store or answering a few staff questions, take more out of me than they should. Even carrying groceries in from the car feels like a lot sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I get foggy over things that should be simple, like deciding which endcap to reset first at work, or even picking out a blouse to wear. I’ll just stand there second-guessing myself, and it takes longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… lately I’ve been feeling guilty over small things. I keep replaying little mistakes at work, like if a display sign is crooked or a trainee looks confused, and then I start telling myself I’m a useless supervisor. It’s probably more than the situation really deserves, but it sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, a little. Some days I notice I’m pacing around the living room and picking at my cuticles, like I can’t quite settle. Other days it’s the opposite—just walking from the break room to the sales floor feels oddly slow, like my body’s dragging.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Somewhat, yes. I’m still going to work and taking care of the usual things, but it’s making parts of the day harder. I’ve been avoiding extra conversations with staff, and I put off simple decisions at the store because I don’t really trust my judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: If the problems with your district manager were resolved, do you think your mood would return to normal?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. It might help some, because that situation has been weighing on me, but I don’t know that everything would just go back to normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say very anxious. I’ve been worried some, yes, but I don’t know that it feels extreme. It kind of comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Even on days when nothing is happening with your district manager, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when nothing’s going on with him, most mornings lately I still wake up with this heavy, gray feeling. It’s like the day already feels drained before I even get dressed for work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Some, yes. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the color’s already drained out of the day before I even get dressed for the store. It’s hard not to feel discouraged when it starts like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F43.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally scared me into making this appointment is… I’ve been having thoughts like, if I didn’t wake up tomorrow, maybe that would be easier. I don’t want to hurt myself, but having that thought at all really shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling… like the color’s already drained out of the day before I even get dressed for work.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent period, have there been long stretches of time when you did not feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, before this I had stretches where I felt more like myself. But this low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now. It’s long enough that things that usually helped me—like my art classes or morning walks—just haven’t really been doing much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still try to do the things I usually like—like setting out my paints or going to pottery class—but once I’m there, I just kind of sit and stare at everything. It doesn’t give me that spark or enjoyment it used to.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen around the time this low mood began?\n\nPatient: Yes. It seemed to start around the time things got difficult with the new district manager. The whole up-and-down with my mood and energy has been going on about two months now, since that trouble really started.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that this low mood is mainly because of the problems with your new district manager?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it’s mainly because of him. The problems with the district manager definitely seemed to line up with when I started feeling worse, but I don’t know if that’s the whole reason.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I can’t think of another clear stretch before this where I felt depressed like this for two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your sleep?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a clear change in my sleep. It’s not something that stands out to me, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed much change in my appetite. It seems about the same to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Even if I’ve been in bed all night, I still feel worn out. Small things at work, like opening the store or answering a few staff questions, take more out of me than they should. Even carrying groceries in from the car feels like a lot sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I get foggy over things that should be simple, like deciding which endcap to reset first at work, or even picking out a blouse to wear. I’ll just stand there second-guessing myself, and it takes longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… lately I’ve been feeling guilty over small things. I keep replaying little mistakes at work, like if a display sign is crooked or a trainee looks confused, and then I start telling myself I’m a useless supervisor. It’s probably more than the situation really deserves, but it sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, a little. Some days I notice I’m pacing around the living room and picking at my cuticles, like I can’t quite settle. Other days it’s the opposite—just walking from the break room to the sales floor feels oddly slow, like my body’s dragging.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Somewhat, yes. I’m still going to work and taking care of the usual things, but it’s making parts of the day harder. I’ve been avoiding extra conversations with staff, and I put off simple decisions at the store because I don’t really trust my judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: If the problems with your district manager were resolved, do you think your mood would return to normal?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. It might help some, because that situation has been weighing on me, but I don’t know that everything would just go back to normal right away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say very anxious. I’ve been worried some, yes, but I don’t know that it feels extreme. It kind of comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Even on days when nothing is happening with your district manager, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when nothing’s going on with him, most mornings lately I still wake up with this heavy, gray feeling. It’s like the day already feels drained before I even get dressed for work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Some, yes. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the color’s already drained out of the day before I even get dressed for the store. It’s hard not to feel discouraged when it starts like that.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been worrying excessively about a number of different things?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not about a lot of different things. I worry some, like anyone, but it hasn’t felt like I’m constantly worrying about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your depression has been stronger than you would normally expect from the problems with your district manager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I mean, the situation with him doesn’t help, but this feels bigger than that. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the day’s already drained before I even get ready for the store.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

This narrows away from F43.2.","is_final":true}
